=== PROMPT VARIANT ===

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
For a non-empty string T consisting of A, B, and C, we call it a good string if it can be turned into an empty string by performing the following two types of operations any number of times in any order.

- Operation 1: Choose two identical characters in the string and delete them (cannot be performed if there are not two or more identical characters).
- Operation 2: Choose one A, one B, and one C in the string and delete them (cannot be performed if there are not one or more of each of A, B, and C).

For example, ABACA is a good string because it can be turned into an empty string by performing the operations as follows:

- Choose the 2nd, 4th, and 5th characters and delete them (Operation 2). The string becomes AA.
- Choose the 1st and 2nd characters and delete them (Operation 1). The string becomes an empty string.

You are given a string S of length N consisting of A, B, C, and ?. How many ways are there to replace each ? with A, B, or C to form a string that contains at least K good strings as contiguous substrings? Substrings are counted separately if they are at different positions in the original string, even if they are identical strings.
Find the count modulo 998244353.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N K
S

Output

Print the answer modulo 998244353.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 50
- 0 \leq K \leq \frac{N(N+1)}{2}
- N and K are integers.
- |S| = N
- S is a string consisting of A, B, C, and ?.

Sample Input 1

4 2
A?AB

Sample Output 1

1

By replacing ? with A, B, or C, we can obtain the following three strings: AAAB, ABAB, ACAB.
Among these, AAAB contains two good substrings: the AA at positions 1,2 and the AA at positions 2,3. Note that even if the substrings are identical as strings, they are counted separately if they are at different positions in the original string.
On the other hand, ABAB contains only one good substring ABAB. Also, ACAB contains only one good substring CAB.

Sample Input 2

50 411
??AB??C???????????????????????????????A???C????A??

Sample Output 2

457279314

Print the count modulo 998244353.

Sample Input 3

1 0
A

Sample Output 3

1

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

def score(code):
    c0 = code % B
    c1 = (code // B) % B
    c2 = (code // B2) % B
    c3 = (code // B3) % B
    return c0*(c0-1)//2 + ...

Let's write code:

import sys
MOD = 998244353
B = 52
POW = [1, B, B*B, B*B*B]
# letter labels: A=1, B=2, C=3
label = {'A':1, 'B':2, 'C':3}

def add_mod(d, key, val):
    d[key] = (d.get(key, 0) + val) % MOD

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N,K = map(int,input().split())
    S = input().strip()
    dp = [dict() for _ in range(4)]
    dp[0][1] = 1  # c0=1
    for ch in S:
        ndp = [dict() for _ in range(4)]
        if ch == '?':
            for s in range(4):
                d = dp[s]
                if not d: continue
                # next states all != s
                for code, val in d.items():
                    for t in range(4):
                        if t == s: continue
                        nc = code + POW[t]
                        nd = ndp[t]
                        nd[nc] = (nd.get(nc, 0) + val) % MOD
        else:
            v = label[ch]
            for s in range(4):
                d = dp[s]
                if not d: continue
                t = s ^ v
                nd = ndp[t]
                add = POW[t]
                for code, val in d.items():
                    nc = code + add
                    nd[nc] = (nd.get(nc, 0) + val) % MOD
        dp = ndp
    ans = 0
    for s in range(4):
        for code, val in dp[s].items():
            c0 = code % B
            c1 = (code // B) % B
            c2 = (code // (B*B)) % B
            c3 = (code // (B*B*B)) % B
            score = c0*(c0-1)//2 + c1*(c1-1)//2 + c2*(c2-1)//2 + c3*(c3-1)//2
            if score >= K:
                ans += val
                if ans >= MOD: ans -= MOD? val < MOD, ans can exceed 2*MOD? If we do ans = (ans+val)%MOD simpler. Number entries ~100k, modulo okay.
    print(ans % MOD)

Need be careful: ans += val; if ans >= MOD: ans -= MOD works only if ans < MOD before and val < MOD, then ans+val < 2MOD, so one subtract enough. We'll maintain ans < MOD. Good.

But in inner loop, using modulo each addition maybe okay. Could optimize by:
nd[nc] = (nd.get(nc, 0) + val) % MOD
If nd[nc] + val may be >=2MOD? nd[nc] < MOD, val < MOD, sum <2MOD, so can do:
old = nd.get(nc, 0) + val
if old >= MOD: old -= MOD
nd[nc] = old
This is faster. We'll use helper? Function call overhead maybe. We'll inline.

For '?' loop:
for s in range(4):
    d=dp[s]
    for code,val in d.items():
        for t in range(4):
            if t == s: continue
            nc = code + POW[t]
            nd = ndp[t]
            old = nd.get(nc, 0) + val
            if old >= MOD: old -= MOD
            nd[nc] = old

Since nd.get returns <MOD, val <MOD, old <2MOD, one subtract enough. Good.

Known char:
t = s ^ v
add = POW[t]
for code,val in d.items():
    nc = code + add
    nd = ndp[t]
    old = nd.get(nc,0)+val
    if old>=MOD: old-=MOD
    nd[nc]=old

Let's maybe write final code now in mind:

import sys

MOD = 998244353
B = 52
POW = [1, B, B*B, B*B*B]
CHAR_TO_LABEL = {'A': 1, 'B': 2, 'C': 3}

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N, K = map(int, input().split())
    S = input().strip()
    if K == 0:
        print(pow(3, S.count('?'), MOD))
        return

    dp = [dict() for _ in range(4)]
    dp[0][1] = 1

    for ch in S:
        ndp = [dict() for _ in range(4)]
        if ch == '?':
            for s in range(4):
                d = dp[s]
                if not d:
                    continue
                for code, val in d.items():
                    # next class can be any except s
                    for t in range(4):
                        if t == s:
                            continue
                        nc = code + POW[t]
                        nd = ndp[t]
                        nv = nd.get(nc, 0) + val
                        if nv >= MOD:
                            nv -= MOD
                        nd[nc] = nv
        else:
            v = CHAR_TO_LABEL[ch]
            for s in range(4):
                d = dp[s]
                if not d:
                    continue
                t = s ^ v
                add = POW[t]
                nd = ndp[t]
                for code, val in d.items():
                    nc = code + add
                    nv = nd.get(nc, 0) + val
                    if nv >= MOD:
                        nv -= MOD
                    nd[nc] = nv
        dp = ndp

    ans = 0
    B2 = B * B
    B3 = B * B * B
    for s in range(4):
        for code, val in dp[s].items():
            c0 = code % B
            c1 = (code // B) % B
            c2 = (code // B2) % B
            c3 = (code // B3) % B
            score = c0 * (c0 - 1) // 2
            score += c1 * (c1 - 1) // 2
            score += c2 * (c2 - 1) // 2
            score += c3 * (c3 - 1) // 2
            if score >= K:
                ans += val
                if ans >= MOD:
                    ans -= MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Need check B3 = B*B*B = 140608. code // B3 gives c3 because code < B^4. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "if not d: continue" if d is empty; okay.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N=50" and "K=0" early return but if S has '?' count maybe 50, pow returns. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "MOD" and "ans" final if ans+val >= MOD subtract once; ans<MOD, val<MOD. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "score" if code has c0 maybe 51, score C(51,2)=1275. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "K" if K=1275 and answer maybe 0; DP will compute. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "good string" if counts all same parity but operation sequence maybe requires deleting pairs after op2; if all even, deleting pairs one by one works. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "prefix class counts" if a class count c includes x0 and xN; pair (0,N) corresponds to whole string. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "class sequence" if x_i = x_{i-1} xor char label; starting x0=0. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "DP over counts" if current state s and counts vector code, but code doesn't include current state explicitly? It does, because count of current state is at least 1 in code. But we don't decode to know current state; we have s separately. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "code" if two different count vectors can have same code due to base? No, base 52 with digits <52 is unique. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "B=52" and "POW" if count digit can be 51; base 52 enough. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "if K==0 early return" and if K=0 but S has '?' count maybe 50, answer pow(3,50,MOD). Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "sample 2" if K=411 and S length 50, DP output sample. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "N" unused and if input has N K but K maybe 0; early return. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "modulo 998244353" and "pow" built-in. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "for t in range(4): if t == s: continue" for '?' and if s=0, t=1,2,3; if s=1, t=0,2,3; etc. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "known char" if v=1 and s=0, t=1; if s=1, t=0; if s=2, t=3; if s=3, t=2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "CHAR_TO_LABEL" if ch is '?' handled; if ch is 'A','B','C'. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "if old >= MOD: old -= MOD" but if old is exactly MOD and we pop, then if later another contribution y, nd.get returns 0, old=y, store y. Correct. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "ans" final if we skip zero entries; no zero entries. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "score" if code has c0 maybe 0 and c0*(c0-1)//2 = 0; okay.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "N=50" and "B=52" if code values maybe not sorted; no issue.

Now, let's
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
.</think>```python
import sys

MOD = 998244353
B = 52
POW = [1, B, B * B, B * B * B]
CHAR_TO_LABEL = {'A': 1, 'B': 2, 'C': 3}


def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N, K = map(int, input().split())
    S = input().strip()

    if K == 0:
        print(pow(3, S.count('?'), MOD))
        return

    # dp[state][encoded_counts] = number of ways
    # state is the current prefix class, encoded as 0..3.
    dp = [dict() for _ in range(4)]
    dp[0][1] = 1  # initial prefix class 0 appears once

    for ch in S:
        ndp = [dict() for _ in range(4)]

        if ch == '?':
            for s in range(4):
                d = dp[s]
                if not d:
                    continue
                for code, val in d.items():
                    for t in range(4):
                        if t == s:
                            continue
                        nc = code + POW[t]
                        nd = ndp[t]
                        nv = nd.get(nc, 0) + val
                        if nv >= MOD:
                            nv -= MOD
                        if nv:
                            nd[nc] = nv
                        else:
                            nd.pop(nc, None)
        else:
            v = CHAR_TO_LABEL[ch]
            for s in range(4):
                d = dp[s]
                if not d:
                    continue
                t = s ^ v
                add = POW[t]
                nd = ndp[t]
                for code, val in d.items():
                    nc = code + add
                    nv = nd.get(nc, 0) + val
                    if nv >= MOD:
                        nv -= MOD
                    if nv:
                        nd[nc] = nv
                    else:
                        nd.pop(nc, None)

        dp = ndp

    ans = 0
    B2 = B * B
    B3 = B * B * B

    for s in range(4):
        for code, val in dp[s].items():
            c0 = code % B
            c1 = (code // B) % B
            c2 = (code // B2) % B
            c3 = (code // B3) % B

            score = c0 * (c0 - 1) // 2
            score += c1 * (c1 - 1) // 2
            score += c2 * (c2 - 1) // 2
            score += c3 * (c3 - 1) // 2

            if score >= K:
                ans += val
                if ans >= MOD:
                    ans -= MOD

    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
